People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

It just kind of loops in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop thinking about this idea that you're not cut out for your role, even when you try to focus on other things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s hard to shut off. Even when I’m trying to work or do something else, it comes back to me feeling like I’m not really senior material… like I fooled people into thinking I could handle it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel nervous around other people at work or in social situations?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really, I guess. I mean, I’m not that social at work, but I wouldn’t say I’m especially nervous around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. I mean, I usually like grilling on weekends, fishing, putting on a record or whatever. Lately I just kind of… don’t bother. The stuff’s there, I just leave it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I haven’t really been eating much… just a few bites here and there, sometimes skipping supper. My belt feels a bit looser, but I’m not trying to lose weight or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble sleeping due to worrying or feeling overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I’ll wake up too early thinking about client tickets and then I can’t really get back to sleep. Other times I sleep enough, I guess, but still feel pretty worn out.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. Mostly I keep thinking I’m not really cut out for the senior stuff at work. Like I somehow convinced people I could handle more than I actually can. It’s… hard not to beat myself up over it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I get stressed, but not like a sudden wave of fear that peaks like that… not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about things other than work or your ability to perform in your role?\n\nPatient: Not really sure. I mean, normal stuff, I guess… bills, family things sometimes. But it doesn’t feel like the same kind of thing. Mostly I notice it with work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries about not being cut out for your role would stop or significantly decrease if you were to leave your current job or switch to a different role?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Maybe it would ease up some, but… I think the feeling would probably follow me. It’s more that I keep thinking I fooled people into believing I’m more capable than I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and fears are excessive and uncontrollable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think maybe they are excessive. I know I keep going over it more than I should. It’s like I keep beating myself up that I’m not really senior material, like I somehow fooled everyone into thinking I can handle more than I really can. I mean, I usually like grilling on weekends, fishing, putting on a record or whatever. Lately I just kind of… don’t bother. The stuff’s there, I just leave it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I haven’t really been eating much… just a few bites here and there, sometimes skipping supper. My belt feels a bit looser, but I’m not trying to lose weight or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble sleeping due to worrying or feeling overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I’ll wake up too early thinking about client tickets and then I can’t really get back to sleep. Other times I sleep enough, I guess, but still feel pretty worn out.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. Mostly I keep thinking I’m not really cut out for the senior stuff at work. Like I somehow convinced people I could handle more than I actually can. It’s… hard not to beat myself up over it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I get stressed, but not like a sudden wave of fear that peaks like that… not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about things other than work or your ability to perform in your role?\n\nPatient: Not really sure. I mean, normal stuff, I guess… bills, family things sometimes. But it doesn’t feel like the same kind of thing. Mostly I notice it with work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries about not being cut out for your role would stop or significantly decrease if you were to leave your current job or switch to a different role?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Maybe it would ease up some, but… I think the feeling would probably follow me. It’s more that I keep thinking I fooled people into believing I’m more capable than I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and fears are excessive and uncontrollable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think maybe they are excessive. I know I keep going over it more than I should. It’s like I keep beating myself up that I’m not really senior material, like I somehow fooled everyone into thinking I can handle more than I really can. It’s hard to shut off.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks or responsibilities outside of work due to these feelings?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I’m still getting to work and handling the main stuff, but outside of that I’ve kind of pulled back. I double-check myself a lot, and with friends or family I just… don’t really trust my mood or my judgment lately, so I avoid some things.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
